import pytest

import quantities


def test_principal_quantities():
    values = quantities.values()
    assert values["tau_s"] == pytest.approx(16.0108549633)
    assert values["beta"] == pytest.approx(0.6215469546)
    assert values["mean_retention_s"] == pytest.approx(23.0432581249)
    assert values["kernel_h1"][0] == pytest.approx(0.01325)
    assert values["kernel_h2"][0] == pytest.approx(-0.035625)
    assert values["kernel_cross_timing"][0] == pytest.approx(0.048875)
    assert values["trunc_h1"][0] == pytest.approx(0.063625)
    assert values["trunc_h2"][0] == pytest.approx(0.095125)
    assert values["homeostasis"][0] == pytest.approx(0.44025)
    assert values["eligibility_required"][0] == pytest.approx(0.34025)


def test_corrected_truncation_tests():
    values = quantities.values()
    assert values["trunc_h1"][2] == pytest.approx(9.1552734375e-05)
    assert values["trunc_h2"][2] == pytest.approx(9.1552734375e-05)
    assert values["trunc_cross_timing"][2] == pytest.approx(0.02874755859375)

from pathlib import Path


def test_reader_package_has_no_legacy_material():
    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    forbidden = (
        "dopa" + "mine", "dan" + "di", "gst_" + "drift", "galloni_" + "vo2",
        "claims" + ".py", "build_" + "release.py", "sha256" + "sums",
    )
    for path in root.rglob("*"):
        if (not path.is_file() or ".venv" in path.parts or "__pycache__" in path.parts
                or path.suffix in {".npz", ".pdf", ".png"}):
            continue
        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore").lower()
        assert not any(term in text or term in path.name.lower() for term in forbidden)
\end{Verbatim}

\section{Thesis figure generators}
\label{sec:generators}
Four figures in the thesis are drawn by a script kept in the project rather than by an
instrument or by either release archive, and those scripts are printed here so that the whole
of the thesis's own drawing code has one home. They belong to Chapters~2, 4 and~5 and are
independent of the Chapter~7 package above: nothing in this section imports it, and nothing in
it is needed to run the reproduction described earlier.

One command in the project regenerates all four, writing straight into the chapter figure
directories:
\begin{quote}
\texttt{python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py}
\end{quote}
It needs only NumPy, Matplotlib and Pillow, and it runs each generator in its own subprocess:
the three schematics apply the house style, which sets a tight bounding box, whereas the
illumination figure is drawn at matplotlib's defaults with its own larger type, and rcParams
are global.

Two inputs are not reproduced here. The Chapter~4 cycling row recomposes an instrument
export, \texttt{bipolar\_cycling\_stress.png}, a 1064 by 2144 raster that is a
measurement rather than a drawing and so is not embedded in this document; it travels with the
project. The Chapter~5 illumination data is embedded, as \texttt{mrl-ito-illumination.csv} below.
\subsection{figures/README.md}
\index{figures/README.md}
\begin{Verbatim}
# External figure generators

Four thesis figures are drawn by a script kept in this project. One command regenerates all
of them, from the project root:

```bash
python -m pip install -r Reproducibility/figures/requirements.txt
python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py
```

Each generator writes straight into the chapter `Figs` directory the thesis reads, replacing
the supplied file in place. No output directory is created. The figures are already rendered
in the project, so this is optional and is never run during a LaTeX build.

## What is regenerated

| generator | writes into | figures |
|---|---|---|
| `Chapter2/concept_figures.py` | `Chapter2/Figs/` | `fig2_neuron_abstraction.pdf`, `fig2_eligibility_trace.pdf`, `fig2_persistent_volatile.pdf`, `fig2_crossbar_state.pdf` |
| `Chapter4/bipolar_cycling_row.py` | `Chapter4/Figs/` | `bipolar_cycling_row.png` |
| `Chapter4/filament_mechanism.py` | `Chapter4/Figs/` | `filament_mechanism_schematic.pdf` and `.png` |
| `Chapter5/ito_illumination.py` | `Chapter5/Figs/` | `ito_iv_illumination.png` |

**Chapter 2** draws the four conceptual figures: the neuron abstraction, the eligibility
trace, the persistent and volatile state comparison, and the crossbar state. The two panels
carrying curves plot closed forms of the models Chapter 2 introduces — leaky
integrate-and-fire, an exponential synaptic filter, and an exponential against a stretched
relaxation kernel — in arbitrary but stated units. No measured data is involved.

**Chapter 4's cycling row** recomposes an instrument export. `bipolar_cycling_stress.png`,
beside the script, is the source: three stacked panels at 1064 x 2144, an aspect that cannot
share a page with body text. The script crops each panel, scales them to a common height and
sets them side by side. Panel pixels are untouched; the MATLAB figure title is dropped
because the caption now carries it.

**Chapter 4's filament schematic** redraws the vacancy-mediated filament picture of Section
4.1.2 in the house style, across pristine, electroformed and ruptured states. It is a
schematic taken from the literature on this material: no measured geometry, no imaged
filament, no counted defect. Circle positions come from a fixed seed and carry no meaning.
The script asserts that no explanatory text sits inside an axes before it saves.

**Chapter 5's illumination figure** plots dark, illuminated, and subsequent-dark IV sweeps of
an ITO device from `Chapter5/data/ito_illumination.csv`. That CSV is the plotted data: three
forward branches of 251 points each, with their curve labels, extracted from the February
2017 optical campaign on device `res2t#1@1`. The sourcemeter spreadsheets are not
distributed. Nothing is fitted or refitted; the script reads the CSV and draws it. The
illumination source was uncalibrated, which is why the chapter treats the figure as a
qualitative check of optical access and rests the pristine optical reference on the
ellipsometry instead.

## Notes

`common/figstyle.py` holds the shared palette, grid and type sizes — the same settings the
two release archives carry, so a schematic redrawn here cannot drift from the plotted figures
around it. It contains presentation only; no number a figure reports comes from it.

`reproduce.py` runs each generator in its own subprocess. The three schematics apply the
house style, which sets a tight bounding box, while the ITO figure is drawn at matplotlib's
defaults with its own larger type. Since rcParams are global, sharing one interpreter would
let whichever generator ran first decide the last one's bounding box.

Figures 3.1 to 3.5 are TikZ inside `Chapter3/chapter3.tex`, so Chapter 3 has no generator
directory. Chapter 6 and Chapter 7 figures are produced by the plotting code inside their
release archives and are not duplicated here.
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/requirements.txt}
\index{figures/requirements.txt}
\begin{Verbatim}
# The external figure generators need only these three.
# Nothing here is required to compile thesis.tex; the figures are already rendered.
matplotlib>=3.7
numpy>=1.24
pillow>=10.0
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/reproduce.py}
\index{figures/reproduce.py}
\begin{Verbatim}
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Regenerate the thesis figures that are drawn by a script in this project.

    python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py

Each generator writes straight into the chapter ``Figs`` directory the thesis reads, so a
run replaces the supplied figure in place and creates no output directory of its own. The
figures are already rendered in the project, so this command is optional: nothing here is
needed to compile ``thesis.tex``.

WHAT IS AND IS NOT HERE. These four generators draw schematics, closed-form model curves,
and one recomposition of an instrument export. Every measured result in the thesis is
recomputed by the two release archives in the directory above, and Figures 3.1 to 3.5 are
TikZ inside ``Chapter3/chapter3.tex`` and need no generator. Figures whose producer is not
retained remain valid supplied assets.

Requires NumPy, Matplotlib and Pillow. See ``requirements.txt``.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import subprocess
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = HERE.parents[1]

#: Each entry is one generator and the chapter figures it writes.
GENERATORS = (
    ("Chapter2/concept_figures.py", "Chapter2/Figs", (
        "fig2_neuron_abstraction.pdf", "fig2_eligibility_trace.pdf",
        "fig2_persistent_volatile.pdf", "fig2_crossbar_state.pdf")),
    ("Chapter4/bipolar_cycling_row.py", "Chapter4/Figs", (
        "bipolar_cycling_row.png",)),
    ("Chapter4/filament_mechanism.py", "Chapter4/Figs", (
        "filament_mechanism_schematic.pdf", "filament_mechanism_schematic.png")),
    ("Chapter5/ito_illumination.py", "Chapter5/Figs", (
        "ito_iv_illumination.png",)),
)


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    failed = []
    for script, figs_dir, outputs in GENERATORS:
        print(f"{script}")
        started = time.perf_counter()
        # ONE SUBPROCESS EACH, deliberately. These generators do not share a style: the three
        # schematics call the house ``apply_style``, which sets ``savefig.bbox`` to tight, while
        # the ITO figure is drawn at matplotlib's defaults with its own larger type. rcParams are
        # global, so running them in one interpreter let whichever ran first decide the last one's
        # bounding box, and the ITO figure came out 1293x840 instead of 1332x880. A subprocess
        # makes each generator behave exactly as it does when run on its own.
        done = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(HERE / script)],
                              capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=str(ROOT))
        if done.stdout.strip():
            print("\n".join("  " + line if not line.startswith("  ") else line
                            for line in done.stdout.rstrip().splitlines()))
        if done.returncode != 0:
            failed.append((script, f"exited {done.returncode}"))
            print(f"  FAILED: exited {done.returncode}")
            for line in (done.stderr or "").rstrip().splitlines()[-3:]:
                print(f"    {line}")
            continue
        missing = [name for name in outputs if not (ROOT / figs_dir / name).is_file()]
        if missing:
            failed.append((script, f"did not write {missing}"))
            print(f"  FAILED: did not write {missing}")
            continue
        print(f"  ok in {time.perf_counter() - started:.1f} s")

    total = len(GENERATORS)
    print(f"\n{total - len(failed)}/{total} generators ran; "
          f"{sum(len(o) for _, _, o in GENERATORS)} figure files written into the chapter "
          f"Figs directories")
    for script, why in failed:
        print(f"  {script}: {why}")
    return 1 if failed else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/common/figstyle.py}
\index{figures/common/figstyle.py}
\begin{Verbatim}
"""The thesis house figure style, as far as the external generators need it.

One palette and one grid are shared by every drawn figure in the thesis, so a schematic
redrawn here cannot drift from the plotted figures around it. The Chapter 6 and Chapter 7
release archives carry their own copy of the same settings; this module is the copy the
generators in this directory use, and it holds only what they call.

Everything below is presentation. No number a figure reports comes from here.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

PALETTE = {
    "device": "#22a884",
    "exponential": "#414487",
    "linear": "#297d8d",
    "control": "#9aa6b2",
    "reference": "#c0392b",
    "gold": "#9bd746",
    "ink": "#2b2b2b",
    "grid": "#c8d0d8",
    "violet": "#423078",
    "slate": "#7f7f7f",
    "rose": "#34618b",
    "pale": "#8d99a6",
    "reward": "#431563",
    "copper": "#6aca5a",
    "teal": "#46b96f",
    "guide": "#8a949f",
}

INK = PALETTE["ink"]
GRID = PALETTE["grid"]

#: Semantic names for the two device schematics. Drawn from the one palette above.
ON = PALETTE["device"]            # the low-resistance state
OFF = PALETTE["pale"]             # the high-resistance state it returns to
LIMIT = PALETTE["reference"]      # a compliance ceiling or threshold
VACANCY = PALETTE["linear"]       # oxygen vacancies in the filament schematic
OXYGEN = PALETTE["exponential"]   # the returning oxygen species

#: Layer fills taken from the xcolor mixes of Figure 3.1, so the drawn stack and the
#: TikZ stack in Chapter 3 agree: black!35 molybdenum, blue!12 silicon oxide,
#: orange!45 titanium, yellow!60 gold.
MO = "#a6a6a6"
SIOX = "#e0e0ff"
TI = "#ffc68c"
AU = "#ffff66"

#: For a schematic that names no layer material.
OXIDE, METAL = "#f2f4f6", "#d9dee3"

#: The thesis text block is 6.09 in. Schematics are drawn at that width and never scaled,
#: so the type prints at the size it is set.
TEXT_WIDTH_IN = 6.09
TITLE, LABEL, NOTE = 7.4, 6.9, 6.6


def apply_style() -> None:
    """Set the shared rcParams. Called once by each generator before it draws."""
    import matplotlib as mpl

    mpl.rcParams.update({
        "figure.dpi": 120,
        "figure.facecolor": "white",
        "savefig.dpi": 400,
        "savefig.facecolor": "white",
        "savefig.bbox": "tight",
        "savefig.pad_inches": 0.02,
        "font.family": "sans-serif",
        "font.sans-serif": ["DejaVu Sans", "Helvetica", "Arial", "sans-serif"],
        "mathtext.fontset": "dejavusans",
        "font.size": 7.8,
        "axes.labelsize": 7.8,
        "axes.titlesize": 8.6,
        "legend.fontsize": 7.4,
        "xtick.labelsize": 7.4,
        "ytick.labelsize": 7.4,
        "figure.titlesize": 8.6,
        "axes.titleweight": "normal",
        "axes.labelpad": 2.5,
        "xtick.major.pad": 2.0,
        "ytick.major.pad": 2.0,
        "xtick.major.size": 3.0,
        "ytick.major.size": 3.0,
        "xtick.minor.size": 1.8,
        "ytick.minor.size": 1.8,
        "axes.grid": True,
        "axes.axisbelow": True,
        "grid.color": PALETTE["grid"],
        "grid.linewidth": 0.5,
        "grid.alpha": 1.0,
        "grid.linestyle": "-",
        "axes.spines.top": False,
        "axes.spines.right": False,
        "axes.linewidth": 0.8,
        "lines.linewidth": 1.8,
        "lines.solid_capstyle": "round",
        "xtick.direction": "out",
        "ytick.direction": "out",
        "legend.frameon": False,
        "legend.borderaxespad": 0.4,
        "legend.handlelength": 1.6,
        "legend.handletextpad": 0.5,
        "legend.columnspacing": 1.0,
        "legend.labelspacing": 0.35,
    })


def house_grid(ax):
    """The one grid. Returns ``ax``, so it can close a chain of style calls."""
    ax.grid(True, color=PALETTE["grid"], linewidth=0.5, alpha=1.0)
    ax.set_axisbelow(True)
    return ax


def panel(ax, letter: str, *, fontsize: float = 8.6):
    """The panel letter, set as the axes title so it is never a floating text.

    The letter only, left aligned, bold. Descriptive text belongs in the caption, so this
    takes no description. ``"(a)"``, ``"(a) "`` and ``"a."`` all normalise to a bare ``a``,
    because one bracketing serves the whole figure set. Returns ``ax`` so a caller can
    write ``bare(panel(ax, "a"))``.
    """
    ax.set_title(str(letter).strip("(). "), loc="left", fontweight="bold", fontsize=fontsize)
    return house_grid(ax)


def _mix(colour: str, other: tuple, fraction: float) -> str:
    """Blend ``colour`` towards ``other`` by ``fraction``, in the manner of an xcolor mix."""
    rgb = tuple(int(colour.lstrip("#")[i:i + 2], 16) for i in (0, 2, 4))
    out = tuple(round(c * (1 - fraction) + o * fraction) for c, o in zip(rgb, other))
    return "#%02x%02x%02x" % out


def tint(colour: str, fraction: float = 0.62) -> str:
    """A lighter version of a palette colour, for a marker fill under a darker outline."""
    return _mix(colour, (255, 255, 255), fraction)


def shade(colour: str, fraction: float = 0.25) -> str:
    """A darker version of a palette colour, for the outline of a tinted marker."""
    return _mix(colour, (0, 0, 0), fraction)


def floating_texts(ax, *, allow=()) -> list:
    """Free text artists drawn inside ``ax``'s frame.

    House rule: nothing explanatory sits inside an axes. Layer names go outside the frame,
    process words on the arrows between panels, and species in a figure-level legend. Axis
    labels, tick labels and the panel letter belong to the axis or the title and are not
    reported, so what comes back is a floating note. ``allow`` exempts exact strings.

    The filament schematic asserts this returns empty before it saves.
    """
    from matplotlib.transforms import Bbox

    exempt = {str(item).strip() for item in allow}
    figure = ax.get_figure()
    figure.canvas.draw()
    renderer = figure.canvas.get_renderer()
    frame = ax.get_window_extent(renderer)
    found = []
    for text in ax.texts:
        if not text.get_visible() or not str(text.get_text()).strip():
            continue
        if str(text.get_text()).strip() in exempt:
            continue
        try:
            box = text.get_window_extent(renderer)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if Bbox.intersection(box, frame) is not None:
            found.append(text)
    return found
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/Chapter2/concept\_figures.py}
\index{figures/Chapter2/concept\_figures.py}
\begin{Verbatim}
"""The four conceptual figures of Chapter 2, in the house style.

Produces ``fig2_neuron_abstraction.pdf``, ``fig2_eligibility_trace.pdf``,
``fig2_persistent_volatile.pdf`` and ``fig2_crossbar_state.pdf`` into ``Chapter2/Figs/``.

Real axes with ticks, tick labels and a grid, a compact legend inside the axes, and
descriptive text left to the caption rather than written on the canvas. Two panels per
figure carry a computed curve; the schematic panels carry only the labels a reader needs
to follow the wiring.

Nothing here is measured data. These are the closed forms of the models introduced in
Chapter 2 (leaky integrate-and-fire, an exponential synaptic filter, an exponential and a
stretched relaxation kernel), plotted in arbitrary but stated units. Every measured number
in this thesis lives in Chapters 4 to 7, and none of it is recomputed here.

The canvas width matches the thesis text width, 437.46 pt = 6.05 in, so each figure is
placed at ``\\textwidth`` with no scaling and the house 7.8 pt type prints at 7.8 pt.

Run from the project root:  python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py
"""
import sys
from pathlib import Path

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = HERE.parents[2]                    # the thesis project root
sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE.parent / "common"))

import numpy as np
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from matplotlib.patches import FancyArrowPatch, FancyBboxPatch, Ellipse, Circle

from figstyle import PALETTE, apply_style, panel

apply_style()

OUT = ROOT / "Chapter2" / "Figs"
W = 6.05                                  # thesis \textwidth, inches

DEV = PALETTE["device"]                   # measured / physical device
EXP = PALETTE["exponential"]              # abstract exponential comparator
INK = PALETTE["ink"]
CTRL = PALETTE["control"]
REF = PALETTE["reference"]
VIO = PALETTE["violet"]
GRID = PALETTE["grid"]


def bare(ax):
    """A schematic panel: keep the panel letter, drop the axes furniture."""
    ax.grid(False)
    ax.set_xticks([])
    ax.set_yticks([])
    for side in ("top", "right", "bottom", "left"):
        ax.spines[side].set_visible(False)
    return ax


def box(ax, x, y, w, h, label, face, sub=None):
    ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((x, y), w, h, boxstyle="round,pad=0.012",
                                linewidth=0.7, edgecolor=INK, facecolor=face))
    ax.text(x + w / 2, y + h * (0.60 if sub else 0.5), label, ha="center",
            va="center", fontsize=7.4, color=INK)
    if sub:
        ax.text(x + w / 2, y + h * 0.26, sub, ha="center", va="center",
                fontsize=6.8, color="#5a6470")


def spike_glyph(ax, cx, cy, w, h, colour=CTRL, lw=0.7):
    """A small action-potential trace: rise, overshoot, undershoot, return."""
    x = np.array([0.00, 0.26, 0.36, 0.44, 0.56, 0.74, 1.00])
    y = np.array([0.00, 0.00, 1.00, -0.42, -0.14, 0.00, 0.00])
    ax.plot(cx + (x - 0.5) * w, cy + y * h, color=colour, lw=lw,
            solid_joinstyle="round")


def arrow(ax, p, q, colour=INK, lw=0.9):
    ax.add_patch(FancyArrowPatch(p, q, arrowstyle="-|>", mutation_scale=7,
                                 linewidth=lw, color=colour,
                                 shrinkA=1.5, shrinkB=1.5))


def key(ax, handles, labels):
    """A one-line key above the axes, right aligned so it clears the panel letter.

    ``panel`` sets the letter as a left-aligned axes title, so anchoring the key to the
    right of the same row puts the two side by side instead of on top of each other. The
    entries name curves that would otherwise be labelled on the canvas, over the data.
    """
    ax.legend(handles, labels, loc="lower right", bbox_to_anchor=(1.0, 1.0),
              ncol=len(handles), frameon=False, fontsize=7.0, handlelength=1.3,
              handletextpad=0.4, columnspacing=1.0, borderaxespad=0.0)


# ===========================================================================
# 2.1  the input pipeline: signalling -> synaptic filter -> membrane
# ===========================================================================
def fig_neuron():
    """Read left to right, in causal order: spikes arrive, the synapse filters them
    into a current, the current charges a membrane that fires and resets."""
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(W, 2.15))
    gs = fig.add_gridspec(1, 3, width_ratios=[1.32, 1.0, 1.0], wspace=0.38,
                          left=0.078, right=0.986, top=0.82, bottom=0.185)

    # -- (a) neuron anatomy, after Chapter 2's reference illustration ------
    ax = bare(panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 0]), "a"))
    ax.set_xlim(0, 1); ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    SOMA_F, SOMA_E, NUC = "#c9744a", "#9d5028", "#f2c265"
    MYE, AXN = "#f6cf7e", "#b4633c"
    cx, cy, rw, rh = 0.235, 0.545, 0.088, 0.125

    # tapering, forked dendrites; drawn before the soma so they tuck under it
    for ang, npulse in ((58, 0), (108, 2), (152, 3), (200, 0), (248, 2), (298, 0)):
        r = np.deg2rad(ang)
        ux, uy = np.cos(r), np.sin(r) * 1.42
        n = np.hypot(ux, uy); ux, uy = ux / n, uy / n
        px, py = -uy, ux
        mid = (cx + ux * 0.115, cy + uy * 0.115)
        tip = (cx + ux * 0.215, cy + uy * 0.215)
        ax.plot([cx, mid[0]], [cy, mid[1]], color=SOMA_F, lw=2.1,
                solid_capstyle="round", zorder=1)
        ax.plot([mid[0], tip[0]], [mid[1], tip[1]], color=SOMA_F, lw=1.2,
                solid_capstyle="round", zorder=1)
        for sgn in (-1, 1):                       # a fork at each tip
            ax.plot([tip[0], tip[0] + ux * 0.055 + px * sgn * 0.055],
                    [tip[1], tip[1] + uy * 0.055 + py * sgn * 0.055],
                    color=SOMA_F, lw=0.8, solid_capstyle="round", zorder=1)
        for k in range(npulse):                   # pulses arriving along it
            f = 0.148 + 0.046 * k
            bx, by = cx + ux * f, cy + uy * f
            ax.plot([bx - px * 0.028, bx + px * 0.028],
                    [by - py * 0.040, by + py * 0.040],
                    color=INK, lw=1.0, zorder=4)

    # the soma, an organic blob, with its nucleus
    th = np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, 240)
    rr = 1.0 + 0.085 * np.sin(5 * th + 0.6) + 0.055 * np.cos(3 * th)
    ax.fill(cx + rw * rr * np.cos(th), cy + rh * rr * np.sin(th),
            facecolor=SOMA_F, edgecolor=SOMA_E, lw=0.7, zorder=2)
    ax.add_patch(Ellipse((cx - 0.004, cy), 0.046, 0.062, facecolor=NUC,
                         edgecolor="none", zorder=3))

    # myelinated axon: capsules separated by nodes, then the terminal
    x0 = cx + 0.085
    for k in range(4):
        xs = x0 + k * 0.135
        ax.plot([xs, xs + 0.135], [cy, cy], color=AXN, lw=1.5, zorder=1)
        ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((xs + 0.014, cy - 0.036), 0.104, 0.072,
                                    boxstyle="round,pad=0.006,rounding_size=0.034", linewidth=0.6,
                                    edgecolor=AXN, facecolor=MYE, zorder=2))
    xe = x0 + 4 * 0.135
    for dy in (0.115, 0.0, -0.115):               # branching axon terminal
        ax.plot([xe, xe + 0.058], [cy, cy + dy], color=SOMA_F, lw=1.1,
                solid_capstyle="round", zorder=1)
        ax.plot([xe + 0.058], [cy + dy], marker="o", ms=2.6, color=SOMA_F,
                zorder=2)
    xmid = (x0 + xe) / 2
    arrow(ax, (x0 + 0.12, 0.740), (xe - 0.05, 0.740), lw=0.8)
    ax.text(xmid, 0.775, "nerve impulse", ha="center", va="bottom",
            fontsize=6.2, color=INK)
    spike_glyph(ax, xmid, 0.930, 0.115, 0.058, colour=INK, lw=0.8)
    # leader lines into the structures they name, after the reference figure
    ax.plot([0.468, 0.240], [0.618, 0.575], color=INK, lw=0.5)
    ax.text(0.475, 0.602, "nucleus", ha="left", va="bottom", fontsize=6.2,
            color=INK)
    ax.plot([0.945, 0.922], [0.370, 0.428], color=INK, lw=0.5)
    ax.text(1.00, 0.315, "synapses", ha="right", va="top", fontsize=6.2,
            color=INK)
    ax.text(0.015, 0.09, "dendrites", ha="left", fontsize=6.2, color=INK)
    ax.text(0.545, 0.09, "myelinated axon", ha="center", fontsize=6.2, color=INK)

    # -- (b) the synapse filters the spike train ---------------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 1]), "b")
    taus, t = 6.0, np.arange(0, 70, 0.05)
    pre = np.array([8.0, 20.0, 26.0, 46.0])
    j = np.zeros_like(t)
    for sp in pre:
        m = t >= sp
        j[m] += np.exp(-(t[m] - sp) / taus)
    j /= j.max()
    h_j, = ax.plot(t, j, color=EXP, lw=1.3, label="$J(t)$")
    for sp in pre:
        ax.plot([sp, sp], [1.16, 1.38], color=INK, lw=1.1)
    ax.plot([0, 70], [1.16, 1.16], color=CTRL, lw=0.6, zorder=1)
    ax.text(35, 1.52, "spikes in", ha="center", va="center", fontsize=6.8, color=INK)
    ax.set_xlim(0, 70); ax.set_ylim(-0.04, 1.66)
    ax.set_xlabel("time, $t$ (ms)")
    ax.set_ylabel("$J$ (norm.)", labelpad=2)
    # The curve label sits on the title row, right of the panel letter, so it names the
    # trace without standing over the decay it labels.
    key(ax, [h_j], ["$J(t)$"])

    # -- (c) the membrane integrates, fires and resets ---------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 2]), "c")
    tau, dt, drive, vth = 11.0, 0.05, 1.5, 1.0
    t = np.arange(0, 70, dt)
    v = np.zeros_like(t); out = []
    ref, held = 5.0, -1e9
    for i in range(1, t.size):
        if t[i] - held < ref:
            v[i] = 0.0
            continue
        v[i] = v[i - 1] + dt / tau * (drive - v[i - 1])
        if v[i] >= vth:
            v[i] = vth; out.append(t[i]); held = t[i]
    h_th = ax.axhline(vth, color=REF, lw=0.9, ls="--", label="$V_{th}$")
    h_v, = ax.plot(t, v, color=DEV, lw=1.3, label="$V(t)$")
    for sp in out:
        ax.plot([sp, sp], [1.16, 1.38], color=INK, lw=1.1)
    ax.plot([0, 70], [1.16, 1.16], color=CTRL, lw=0.6, zorder=1)
    ax.text(35, 1.52, "spikes out", ha="center", va="center", fontsize=6.8, color=INK)
    ax.set_xlim(0, 70); ax.set_ylim(-0.04, 1.66)
    ax.set_xlabel("time, $t$ (ms)")
    ax.set_ylabel("$V/V_{th}$", labelpad=2)
    # Membrane first, then the threshold it crosses, which is the order the panel is read
    # in rather than the order the two artists were drawn.
    key(ax, [h_v, h_th], ["$V(t)$", "$V_{th}$"])

    fig.savefig(OUT / "fig2_neuron_abstraction.pdf")
    plt.close(fig)


# ===========================================================================
# 2.2  three-factor learning and the eligibility trace
# ===========================================================================
def fig_eligibility():
    """One generic fading trace. The abstract rule only requires that eligibility
    decay; which decay a device actually supplies is a later question."""
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(W, 3.05))
    gs = fig.add_gridspec(3, 1, height_ratios=[0.42, 1.45, 0.62], hspace=0.30,
                          left=0.105, right=0.985, top=0.93, bottom=0.125)
    t = np.linspace(0, 120, 2400)
    t0, tR, tau_e = 12.0, 55.0, 34.0

    # -- (a) spike rasters --------------------------------------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 0]), "a")
    ax.grid(False)
    for sp in (5.0, 9.0, 12.0):
        ax.plot([sp, sp], [0.58, 0.96], color=INK, lw=1.0)
    for sp in (6.6, 10.4, 12.9):
        ax.plot([sp, sp], [0.06, 0.44], color=CTRL, lw=1.0)
    ax.set_xlim(0, 120); ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    ax.set_yticks([0.25, 0.77]); ax.set_yticklabels(["post", "pre"], fontsize=6.8)
    ax.set_xticklabels([])
    for side in ("top", "right"):
        ax.spines[side].set_visible(False)

    # -- (b) the fading eligibility trace -----------------------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[1, 0]), "b")
    e = np.where(t < t0, 0.0, np.exp(-(t - t0) / tau_e))
    ax.plot(t, e, color=EXP, lw=1.4)
    ax.axvline(tR, color=CTRL, lw=0.8, ls=":")
    ax.plot([tR], [np.exp(-(tR - t0) / tau_e)], "o", ms=3.8, color=REF, zorder=5)
    ax.annotate("reward, $t_R$", xy=(tR, 1.02), xytext=(tR + 5.5, 1.02),
                fontsize=6.8, color=REF, va="center",
                arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle="-|>", color=REF, lw=0.9,
                                mutation_scale=7, shrinkA=1, shrinkB=1))
    ax.text(tR + 5.5, np.exp(-(tR - t0) / tau_e) + 0.02, "$e_{ij}(t_R)$",
            fontsize=7.0, color=REF, va="bottom")
    ax.text(t0 + 5.5, 0.86, "$e_{ij}(t)$", fontsize=7.4, color=EXP)
    ax.set_xlim(0, 120); ax.set_ylim(-0.04, 1.13)
    ax.set_ylabel("eligibility (norm.)"); ax.set_xticklabels([])

    # -- (c) the persistent weight -----------------------------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[2, 0]), "c")
    w = np.where(t < tR, 0.22, 0.72)
    ax.plot(t, w, color=VIO, lw=1.4)
    ax.axvline(tR, color=CTRL, lw=0.8, ls=":")
    ax.set_xlim(0, 120); ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    ax.set_xlabel("time since coincidence, $t$ (arbitrary units)")
    ax.set_ylabel("weight, $w$"); ax.set_yticks([0.22, 0.72])
    ax.set_yticklabels(["before", "after"], fontsize=6.8)

    fig.savefig(OUT / "fig2_eligibility_trace.pdf")
    plt.close(fig)


# ===========================================================================
# 2.3  persistent and volatile memristive state
# ===========================================================================
def fig_states():
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(W, 2.05))
    gs = fig.add_gridspec(1, 2, wspace=0.26, left=0.075, right=0.985,
                          top=0.85, bottom=0.20)
    t = np.linspace(0, 120, 2400)

    # -- (a) persistent -----------------------------------------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 0]), "a")
    g = np.select([t < 25, t < 62], [0.22, 0.52], 0.82)
    ax.plot(t, g, color=VIO, lw=1.4)
    for s in (25.0, 62.0):
        ax.annotate("write", xy=(s, 0.97), xytext=(s, 1.08), fontsize=6.8,
                    ha="center", color=REF,
                    arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle="-|>", color=REF, lw=0.8,
                                    mutation_scale=6, shrinkA=0.5, shrinkB=0.5))
    ax.set_xlim(0, 120); ax.set_ylim(0, 1.22)
    ax.set_xlabel("time, $t$ (arbitrary units)")
    ax.set_ylabel("conductance, $G$ (norm.)")

    # -- (b) volatile -------------------------------------------------------
    ax = panel(fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 1]), "b")
    tw, tr, tau = 14.0, 60.0, 26.0
    # The decay is drawn only from the end of the write, and the write itself as the ramp below. Plotting a
    # zero baseline across the write window and letting it jump to unity drew a spurious vertical line at
    # t = tw, which is a step discontinuity joined by the line renderer rather than anything physical.
    hold = t >= tw
    ax.axvspan(0, tw, color=GRID, alpha=0.55, lw=0)
    ax.plot(t[hold], np.exp(-(t[hold] - tw) / tau), color=DEV, lw=1.4)
    ax.plot([0, tw], [0.0, 1.0], color=DEV, lw=1.4)
    ax.text(tw / 2, 1.09, "write", ha="center", fontsize=6.8, color=INK)
    ax.annotate("read", xy=(tr, np.exp(-(tr - tw) / tau)),
                xytext=(tr + 13, 0.72), fontsize=6.8, color=REF,
                arrowprops=dict(arrowstyle="-|>", color=REF, lw=0.8,
                                mutation_scale=6, shrinkA=1, shrinkB=1.5))
    ax.plot([tr], [np.exp(-(tr - tw) / tau)], "o", ms=3.4, color=REF, zorder=5)
    ax.set_xlim(0, 120); ax.set_ylim(0, 1.22)
    ax.set_xlabel("time, $t$ (arbitrary units)")
    ax.set_ylabel("volatile state (norm.)")

    fig.savefig(OUT / "fig2_persistent_volatile.pdf")
    plt.close(fig)


# ===========================================================================
# 2.4  crossbar computation with local device state
# ===========================================================================
def fig_crossbar():
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(W, 2.35))
    ax = bare(fig.add_subplot(111))
    ax.set_position([0.02, 0.02, 0.96, 0.96])
    ax.set_xlim(0, 10); ax.set_ylim(0, 4.2)

    rows = {"$V_1$": 3.42, "$V_2$": 2.62}
    cols = [(1.95, "$G^{+}$"), (2.85, "$G^{-}$"), (4.45, "$G^{+}$"), (5.35, "$G^{-}$")]
    for lab, y in rows.items():
        ax.plot([1.45, 6.05], [y, y], color=INK, lw=0.8)
        ax.text(1.32, y, lab, ha="right", va="center", fontsize=7.4, color=INK)
    for x, lab in cols:
        ax.plot([x, x], [3.80, 1.95], color=INK, lw=0.8)
        ax.text(x, 3.90, lab, ha="center", fontsize=7.0, color=INK)
        for ri, y in enumerate(rows.values(), start=1):
            ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((x - 0.155, y - 0.145), 0.31, 0.29,
                                        boxstyle="round,pad=0.01", linewidth=0.6,
                                        edgecolor=INK, facecolor="#cfd8ee"))
            # the volatile internal state s of Equation 2.5 lives in the same cell as the programmed
            # conductance, so it is drawn inside the device rather than beside it. Without this mark the
            # key's second entry named a quantity the drawing did not show.
            ax.add_patch(Circle((x, y), 0.075, linewidth=0.4, edgecolor=INK,
                                facecolor=DEV, zorder=4))
            # One state per corner of the pair box: horizontally away from its own column line, and above
            # the upper word line or below the lower one, so no label meets the wiring or a dashed border.
            ci = cols.index((x, lab)) + 1
            outward = -1 if ci % 2 else 1
            ax.text(x + outward * 0.185, y + (0.175 if ri == 1 else -0.175),
                    f"$s_{{{ri}{ci}}}$", ha="left" if outward > 0 else "right", va="center",
                    fontsize=6.2, color=DEV, zorder=5)
    # The boxes are wider than the column pitch so that each of the four states can be named in its own
    # corner, outside its column line, without a label crossing a dashed border.
    for x0, lab in ((1.44, "pair 1"), (3.94, "pair 2")):
        ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((x0, 2.20), 2.00, 1.52,
                                    boxstyle="round,pad=0.02", linewidth=0.7,
                                    edgecolor=CTRL, facecolor="none", ls=(0, (3, 2))))
        ax.text(x0 + 1.00, 2.03, lab, ha="center", fontsize=6.8, color="#5a6470")

    for xc, xa, xb in ((2.40, 1.95, 2.85), (4.90, 4.45, 5.35)):
        for x in (xa, xb):
            arrow(ax, (x, 1.95), (x, 1.62), lw=0.8)
            ax.plot([x, xc], [1.62, 1.62], color=INK, lw=0.8)
        box(ax, xc - 0.58, 1.12, 1.16, 0.40, "$I^{+}\\!-\\!I^{-}$", "#eef1f4")
        arrow(ax, (xc, 1.12), (xc, 0.82), lw=0.8)
        box(ax, xc - 0.48, 0.42, 0.96, 0.40, "neuron", "#f2d9b0")
        arrow(ax, (xc, 0.42), (xc, 0.12), lw=0.8)
        ax.text(xc, 0.02, "$y$", ha="center", fontsize=7.4, color=INK)

    ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((6.55, 1.55), 3.35, 2.25,
                                boxstyle="round,pad=0.03", linewidth=0.7,
                                edgecolor=CTRL, facecolor="#f7f8fa"))
    entries = [("$G$", "programmed conductance,\nthe persistent weight"),
               ("$s$", "volatile internal state,\nset by recent activity"),
               ("$I$", "bit-line current, $\\mathbf{I}=\\mathbf{G}\\mathbf{V}$"),
               ("$y$", "neural output")]
    y = 3.52
    for sym, txt in entries:
        ax.text(6.72, y, sym, ha="left", va="top", fontsize=7.4, color=INK)
        ax.text(7.38, y, txt, ha="left", va="top", fontsize=6.8, color="#5a6470")
        y -= 0.56 if "\n" in txt else 0.40

    fig.savefig(OUT / "fig2_crossbar_state.pdf")
    plt.close(fig)


def main() -> int:
    OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fig_neuron(); fig_eligibility(); fig_states(); fig_crossbar()
    for f in sorted(OUT.glob("fig2_*.pdf")):
        print(f"  wrote {f.relative_to(ROOT)}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/Chapter4/bipolar\_cycling\_row.py}
\index{figures/Chapter4/bipolar\_cycling\_row.py}
\begin{Verbatim}
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Chapter 4, Figure 4.7: the three bipolar cycling panels as a row instead of a column.

Produces ``bipolar_cycling_row.png`` into ``Chapter4/Figs/``.

SOURCE. ``bipolar_cycling_stress.png``, beside this script, is the instrument export: three stacked
panels at 1064 x 2144. That 1:2 aspect cannot share a page with body text at any useful width, so it
forced a half-empty page. The three panels are independent axes, so nothing is lost by setting them
side by side.

WHAT IS CHANGED, and nothing else. The pixels of each panel are untouched: this producer crops, scales
to a common height, and composes. It also drops the MATLAB figure title, whose content now lives in the
caption, and trims the black compositing strips at the panel edges of the original.

MEASURED GEOMETRY, not guessed. Panel axes frames sit at rows 58-693, 787-1380 and 1468-2063, found from
the long horizontal dark runs. The title occupies rows 14-41 and the topmost right-axis label ``400`` rows
52-66, so cropping above row 49 removes the title and keeps every tick label.

Run from the project root:  python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = HERE.parents[2]                    # the thesis project root
SRC = HERE / "bipolar_cycling_stress.png"
OUT = ROOT / "Chapter4" / "Figs" / "bipolar_cycling_row.png"

#: Row bands separating the three panels, taken from the axes frames plus their tick labels.
#: The first band starts below the figure title.
BANDS = ((49, 778), (778, 1466), (1466, 2144))

GAP = 46            # white gutter between panels, in output pixels
PAD = 14            # outer margin
LABEL_H = 76        # band above the panels carrying (a), (b), (c)


def content_box(arr: np.ndarray, y0: int, y1: int) -> tuple[int, int, int, int]:
    """Tight box of real ink in a row band, ignoring the original's black edge strips."""
    lum = arr[y0:y1].mean(axis=2)
    ink, black = lum < 240, lum < 30
    artefact = black.mean(axis=0) > 0.8          # a column that is essentially all black
    ink = ink.copy()
    ink[:, artefact] = False
    # A column or row counts as content only if it carries several ink pixels, so isolated specks
    # left over from the original's panel joins do not widen the box.
    cols = np.where(ink.sum(axis=0) >= 3)[0]
    rows = np.where(ink.sum(axis=1) >= 3)[0]
    return int(cols.min()), int(y0 + rows.min()), int(cols.max()) + 1, int(y0 + rows.max()) + 1


def _font(size: int):
    for name in ("Helvetica.ttc", "Arial.ttf", "DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf"):
        try:
            return ImageFont.truetype(name, size)
        except OSError:
            continue
    return ImageFont.load_default()


def main() -> int:
    OUT.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    src = Image.open(SRC).convert("RGB")
    arr = np.asarray(src).astype(int)

    panels = [src.crop(content_box(arr, y0, y1)) for y0, y1 in BANDS]
    height = max(p.height for p in panels)
    panels = [p.resize((round(p.width * height / p.height), height), Image.LANCZOS) for p in panels]

    width = sum(p.width for p in panels) + GAP * (len(panels) - 1) + 2 * PAD
    out = Image.new("RGB", (width, height + LABEL_H + 2 * PAD), "white")
    font = _font(58)
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(out)

    x = PAD
    for letter, panel in zip("abc", panels):
        out.paste(panel, (x, PAD + LABEL_H))
        draw.text((x + 6, PAD + 4), f"({letter})", fill=(20, 20, 20), font=font)
        x += panel.width + GAP

    out.save(OUT, dpi=(400, 400))
    print(f"wrote {OUT.relative_to(ROOT)}  {out.size[0]} x {out.size[1]}  aspect {out.size[0]/out.size[1]:.2f}:1")
    print("  panel widths:", [p.width for p in panels], "common height:", height)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/Chapter4/filament\_mechanism.py}
\index{figures/Chapter4/filament\_mechanism.py}
\begin{Verbatim}
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Chapter 4: the vacancy-mediated filament picture Section 4.1.2 works with, drawn on this thesis's stack.

REDRAWN, not reused. The original artwork is the author's own, from the transfer thesis, drawn in office
software with a second panel for a titanium-nitride stack that is not studied here. This producer redraws the
one relevant panel in the thesis house style and drops the foreign stack.

DRAWN GENERICALLY. An earlier version drew the Mo / SiOx / Ti / Au stack with its titanium and gold layers
labelled, which presented unipolar operation on the family that switches bipolarly. Both switching families
share this vacancy account, so the top electrode is now one unlabelled-material contact bar. The bottom
electrode stays molybdenum because every family in Chapter 3 has it, and the oxide colour comes from
Figure 3.1 by way of the shared ``figstyle`` module.

WHAT THE THREE STATES SAY, in the order Section 4.1.2 tells it:
  (a) pristine -- oxygen vacancies present but dispersed, so no continuous path carries current;
  (b) electroformed -- a localised vacancy path bridges the oxide, which is the conductance jump at forming;
  (c) ruptured -- the path is broken near one electrode, whether by returning oxygen under the field in the
      bipolar mode or by thermal rupture above a critical current in the unipolar one.

The second process arrow says "rupture" rather than "reset", because reset is the electrical operation and what
the panel draws is what that operation does to the filament.

WORKING INTERPRETATION, NOT AN IDENTIFICATION. The chapter takes the vacancy filament from the literature on
this material rather than from its own fits, so nothing here is a measured geometry, an imaged filament or a
counted defect. Circle positions come from a fixed seed and carry no meaning.

House rule: nothing explanatory sits inside an axes. Layer names sit outside the frame, process words sit on the
arrows between panels in figure coordinates, and the two species are named in a figure-level legend.

Produces ``filament_mechanism_schematic.pdf`` and ``.png`` into ``Chapter4/Figs/``.

Run from the project root:  python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from pathlib import Path

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = HERE.parents[2]                    # the thesis project root
sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE.parent / "common"))

import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import numpy as np
from matplotlib.lines import Line2D
from matplotlib.patches import FancyArrowPatch, Rectangle

import figstyle as S

OUT = ROOT / "Chapter4" / "Figs"

RNG = np.random.default_rng(4)          # fixed, so the drawing is reproducible; positions carry no meaning
OXIDE_TOP, OXIDE_BOT = 0.74, 0.26       # the oxide occupies this band of each panel
MO_H = 0.20                             # molybdenum bar height
TOP_H = 0.24                            # the top contact, drawn as one bar of unstated material
DOT = 17                                # vacancy marker area in points squared, so the dots stay round
EDGE_LW = 0.7                           # a darker outline on a lighter fill, so touching circles stay distinct


def _stack(ax):
    """A generic stack: molybdenum below, SiOx, and one top contact whose material is left unstated."""
    ax.set_axis_off()
    ax.set_xlim(0, 1)
    ax.set_ylim(0, 1)
    for y0, h, fill in ((OXIDE_BOT - MO_H, MO_H, S.MO),
                        (OXIDE_BOT, OXIDE_TOP - OXIDE_BOT, S.SIOX),
                        (OXIDE_TOP, TOP_H, S.METAL)):
        ax.add_patch(Rectangle((0, y0), 1, h, facecolor=fill, edgecolor=S.GRID, lw=0.5, zorder=2))
    return ax


def _dots(ax, xy, colour):
    """Scatter rather than patches, so a vacancy is round whatever the panel's aspect ratio.

    Every vacancy is drawn the same way: a light tint of its palette colour with the full-strength colour
    darkened for the outline. An earlier version faded the ones away from the path to make the filament stand
    out, which left two shades of the same species on the canvas and one legend entry for them. The path is
    legible from its density alone, which is also the physical claim.
    """
    ax.scatter(xy[:, 0], xy[:, 1], s=DOT, facecolors=S.tint(colour), edgecolors=S.shade(colour),
               linewidths=EDGE_LW, zorder=4)


def _scatter(n, x0, x1, y0, y1):
    return np.column_stack([RNG.uniform(x0, x1, n), RNG.uniform(y0, y1, n)])


def _column(n, x, y0, y1, jitter=0.02):
    y = np.linspace(y0, y1, n)
    return np.column_stack([x + RNG.uniform(-jitter, jitter, n), y])


def pristine(ax):
    _stack(ax)
    _dots(ax, _scatter(16, 0.08, 0.92, OXIDE_BOT + 0.04, OXIDE_TOP - 0.04), S.VACANCY)


def electroformed(ax):
    _stack(ax)
    _dots(ax, _scatter(10, 0.08, 0.92, OXIDE_BOT + 0.04, OXIDE_TOP - 0.04), S.VACANCY)
    # Two tight columns spanning the full oxide, so the path visibly bridges the two electrodes.
    for x in (0.475, 0.525):
        _dots(ax, _column(7, x, OXIDE_BOT + 0.005, OXIDE_TOP - 0.005, jitter=0.006), S.VACANCY)


def ruptured(ax):
    _stack(ax)
    _dots(ax, _scatter(10, 0.08, 0.92, OXIDE_BOT + 0.04, OXIDE_TOP - 0.04), S.VACANCY)
    # The stub reaches less than half way, so the break below the top contact is unmistakable.
    for x in (0.475, 0.525):
        _dots(ax, _column(4, x, OXIDE_BOT + 0.005, 0.46, jitter=0.006), S.VACANCY)
    _dots(ax, _scatter(9, 0.41, 0.59, 0.54, OXIDE_TOP - 0.02), S.OXYGEN)


def main() -> int:
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

    S.apply_style()
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(S.TEXT_WIDTH_IN, 1.85))
    for ax, letter, title, draw in zip(axes, "abc", ("pristine", "electroformed", "ruptured"),
                                       (pristine, electroformed, ruptured)):
        draw(ax)
        ax.text(0.0, 1.05, f"({letter}) {title}", ha="left", va="bottom", fontsize=S.TITLE,
                color=S.INK, transform=ax.transAxes)

    # Layer names outside the first frame, so the stack is named without putting text in an axes.
    for y, name in ((OXIDE_TOP + TOP_H / 2, "contact"),
                    ((OXIDE_TOP + OXIDE_BOT) / 2, "SiO$_x$"),
                    (OXIDE_BOT - MO_H / 2, "Mo")):
        axes[0].text(-0.035, y, name, ha="right", va="center", fontsize=S.NOTE, color=S.INK,
                     transform=axes[0].transAxes)

    # Process arrows in the gaps between panels, in figure coordinates. wspace is set wide enough that the word
    # fits inside its gap: at the earlier 0.30 both words overlapped the panels either side.
    fig.subplots_adjust(left=0.085, right=0.995, bottom=0.19, top=0.80, wspace=0.54)
    fig.canvas.draw()
    for left, right, word in ((0, 1, "electroform"), (1, 2, "rupture")):
        b0, b1 = axes[left].get_position(), axes[right].get_position()
        y = (b0.y0 + b0.y1) / 2
        x0, x1 = b0.x1 + 0.010, b1.x0 - 0.010
        fig.add_artist(FancyArrowPatch((x0, y - 0.03), (x1, y - 0.03), transform=fig.transFigure,
                                       arrowstyle="-|>", mutation_scale=7.0, lw=0.9, color=S.INK))
        fig.text((x0 + x1) / 2, y + 0.02, word, ha="center", va="bottom", fontsize=S.NOTE, color=S.INK)

    # The two species named in a figure-level legend below the row, so no axes carries a key.
    handles = [Line2D([], [], marker="o", ls="none", ms=3.8, markerfacecolor=S.tint(S.VACANCY),
                      markeredgecolor=S.shade(S.VACANCY), markeredgewidth=EDGE_LW, label="oxygen vacancy"),
               Line2D([], [], marker="o", ls="none", ms=3.8, markerfacecolor=S.tint(S.OXYGEN),
                      markeredgecolor=S.shade(S.OXYGEN), markeredgewidth=EDGE_LW, label="returned oxygen")]
    fig.legend(handles=handles, loc="lower center", ncol=2, frameon=False, fontsize=S.NOTE,
               handletextpad=0.4, columnspacing=1.8, bbox_to_anchor=(0.54, 0.0))

    for ax in axes:
        stray = S.floating_texts(ax)
        if stray:
            raise AssertionError(f"floating text inside an axes: {[t.get_text() for t in stray]}")
    OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fig.savefig(OUT / "filament_mechanism_schematic.png", dpi=400)
    fig.savefig(OUT / "filament_mechanism_schematic.pdf")
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"  wrote Chapter4/Figs/filament_mechanism_schematic.pdf and .png")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
\end{Verbatim}
\subsection{figures/Chapter5/ito\_illumination.py}
\index{figures/Chapter5/ito\_illumination.py}
\begin{Verbatim}
"""Chapter 5, Figure 5.5: dark, illuminated, and subsequent-dark IV sweeps of an ITO device.

Produces ``ito_iv_illumination.png`` into ``Chapter5/Figs/``.

Nothing is fitted or recomputed here. The script reads the three plotted branches from
``data/ito_illumination.csv`` and draws them; the curves, their axes and their ordering are
the ones the figure has always shown.

DATA SOURCE. The February 2017 optical campaign on device ``res2t#1@1``, recorded as
sourcemeter exports with Time/AI/AV columns:

===========================  ==================================================  ==========
curve                        export                                              sheet
===========================  ==================================================  ==========
dark                         21 IV up to +25 Dark and Laser).xls                 Data
under illumination           21 IV up to +25 Dark and Laser).xls                 Append1
dark (subsequent sweep)      21a after laser in dark +25V.xls                    Data
===========================  ==================================================  ==========

Each export is a dual sweep, 0 to +25 V and back, 502 points. The caption reports the
forward branch, so the CSV carries the ascending half only: it was cut at the turning point
found from the voltage column rather than at the midpoint, because an interrupted sweep does
not turn where the point count says it should. That leaves 251 points per curve. The
spreadsheets are not distributed with the thesis; the CSV is the plotted data.

The illumination source was uncalibrated, which is why the prose treats this figure as a
qualitative check of optical access and rests the pristine optical reference on the
ellipsometry instead. Wavelength and power are not recorded in any export.

Run from the project root:  python Reproducibility/figures/reproduce.py
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import csv
from collections import OrderedDict
from pathlib import Path

import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = HERE.parents[2]                    # the thesis project root
DATA = HERE / "data" / "ito_illumination.csv"
OUT = ROOT / "Chapter5" / "Figs" / "ito_iv_illumination.png"

#: Grey and navy are the two colours the figure has always used; the teal matches the
#: neighbouring Chapter 5 figures ``ito_stress_response.png`` and
#: ``ito_optical_dispersion.png``, both #3aa07a.
COLOURS = {
    "dark": "#9aa6b2",
    "under illumination": "#3aa07a",
    "dark (subsequent sweep)": "#2f4b8f",
}


def branches(path: Path) -> "OrderedDict[str, tuple[list[float], list[float]]]":
    """The forward branches, keyed by curve label, in the order the CSV lists them."""
    out: OrderedDict[str, tuple[list[float], list[float]]] = OrderedDict()
    with path.open(newline="") as handle:
        for row in csv.DictReader(handle):
            volts, amps = out.setdefault(row["curve"], ([], []))
            volts.append(float(row["voltage_v"]))
            amps.append(float(row["current_abs_a"]))
    return out


def main() -> int:
    if not DATA.is_file():
        raise SystemExit(f"plotted data not found: {DATA}")
    curves = branches(DATA)

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(6.66, 4.40))
    for label, (volts, amps) in curves.items():
        ax.plot(volts, amps, color=COLOURS[label], lw=1.6, label=label,
                solid_joinstyle="round")

    ax.set_yscale("log")
    ax.set_xlim(0, 25)
    ax.set_xlabel("Applied voltage (V)", fontsize=13)
    ax.set_ylabel("Current magnitude (A)", fontsize=13)
    ax.grid(True, which="major", color="#c8d0d8", lw=0.6, alpha=0.9)
    ax.grid(True, which="minor", color="#e3e7eb", lw=0.4, alpha=0.9)
    ax.tick_params(labelsize=11)
    for side in ("top", "right"):
        ax.spines[side].set_visible(True)
        ax.spines[side].set_linewidth(0.8)
    ax.legend(loc="lower right", frameon=False, fontsize=12,
              handlelength=1.8, handletextpad=0.7, labelspacing=0.6)

    fig.tight_layout()
    OUT.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fig.savefig(OUT, dpi=200)
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"  wrote {OUT.relative_to(ROOT)}")
    for label, (volts, amps) in curves.items():
        print(f"    {label:<24} {len(volts)} points, "
              f"|I| {min(amps):.2e} to {max(amps):.2e} A, {COLOURS[label]}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
\end{Verbatim}
